    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

oracle ceiling 的 37% (0.0807/0.218)。CED-F1 Spearman r=0.3235 (p≈0)，相关性显著但中等——说明 CED 不是完美的 proxy 但有信号。Random baseline rel_f1=0.4538 说明 sampling diversity 本身就有 +4.8pp 收益，CED 在此基础上再加 +3.3pp。EDCR 下降 (0.6156 vs SFT 0.7118)，N=8 sampling 引入更多 distractor citations。推理耗时 5.8h (N=8 生成主导)。结论：CED 作为 inference-time reranker 有明确论文价值——零训练成本超越 RSFT，且 oracle ceiling 表明更好的 reward model 还有大幅空间。\"}, Now I have a much clearer picture. Let me analyze what the worker did:

**Worker task: fix_seed_comparison_ign_f1**
The worker was tasked with fixing the seed comparison for ign_f1 metrics. It seems like the issue was that earlier evaluations used inconsistent protocols (different batch_size, max_new_tokens, etc.), and some seeds had Ign-F1 computed incorrectly (perhaps the old metrics didn't have a proper Ign-F1 that excludes shared entity pairs).

**What the worker did:**
1. Generated `seed_comparison.md/json` — comparing CED RSFT vs flat-NLI RSFT across seeds


2. Created tracking files for D076-compliant seeds and paper table data, then kicked off a fresh SFT baseline re-evaluation with the unified protocol
3. Multiple re-evaluations are already in progress across different seed and model combinations, with GPU 0 running at 84% capacity handling at least one of these jobs

Now I'm seeing a resource conflict — both reeval_flat_nli_s42 and reeval_ced_s44 are assigned to cuda:0, which explains the high memory usage on GPU 0. I need to check whether this is causing contention or if the memory accounting is just overlapping between the two processes. This violates constraint C001 — I need to check GPU memory is under 2GB before submitting a new eval. When reeval_ced_s44 was submitted at 04:18, cuda:0 already had ~11GB in use from the first eval, which clearly exceeds the 2GB threshold. So the second submission shouldn't have happened, even though 22GB total fits within the 32GB VRAM limit.

But looking at the worker timestamp, `worker_cpu_fix_seed_comparison_ign_f1_20260516T052536` started at 05:25 UTC, which is after both reeval experiments were submitted at 04:16-04:24 UTC. This worker didn't submit those experiments — they came from an earlier process. What I'm seeing is that this worker ran from 05:25 onward and generated the seed_comparison files around that time.

Now I'm checking what this worker actually produced: it created the seed comparison summaries, the d076 seed summary, the paper table data, and submitted the reeval_sft_baseline_d076 registry entry. Let me verify the metrics in paper_table_data.json against what's in the registry to see if there are any inconsistencies.

Looking at the metrics, I'm noticing the seed_comparison.json values match the registry entries for CED s43, s45, and flatNLI s44 when I compare them side by side.

But there's something odd here — CED s43 and s45 only have metrics.json in their records, not metrics_v2.json, yet seed_comparison.py somehow pulled Ign-F1 values for them. I need to trace where those CED Ign-F1 numbers actually came from, since the script should only be reading from metrics_v2.json.

I'm also noticing the seed coverage is thin for a paired comparison — only s45 appears in both CED and flatNLI results.  The comparison file correctly flags that we need at least 2 matched seeds for proper paired statistics, but we're working with just 2 seeds per method, which makes any summary stats pretty unreliable. I should verify the actual metrics files on the server and check what's in paper_table_data.json to understand the GPU setup and resource constraints better.

5. Found that `d076_seed_summary.json` has stale information about CED metrics — it claims there's no `metrics_v2.json` for seeds s43 and s45, but both files actually exist and contain identical data to `metrics.json`.

6. Identified a protocol mismatch where the SFT baseline was evaluated with batch_size=16 while RSFT seeds use batch_size=4 per the D076 protocol. A reeval is already in progress to fix this, but the current `paper_table_data.json` still contains the mismatched baseline metrics, which could lead to incorrect comparisons if used before the reeval completes.

The seed comparison is still too preliminary with only one overlapping seed between methods, making any statistical conclusions impossible at this stage. The worker is appropriately waiting for reevals to finish before drawing conclusions.

Checking GPU allocation across the running experiments shows they're distributed across different devices.  Two experiments are actually running on cuda:0 simultaneously — reeval_flat_nli_s42 and reeval_ced_s44 — which violates the constraint requiring less than 2GB free memory before submission. However, the card is handling it fine with 21706 MiB used (66% of capacity), so while the protocol was breached, the actual execution isn't failing.

Now I'm noticing reeval_sft_baseline_d076 was just submitted to cuda:1 with a PID and timestamp, yet the status shows it's waiting for manual approval. That's odd — it has process information suggesting it's running, but the registry indicates it hasn't been fully approved yet.

Let me verify the metrics from CED s43 against what's recorded. The F1 scores and other performance metrics all match between the metrics files and registry, which suggests the evaluation completed consistently or the fix was applied before the run started.

The key insight is that metrics.json and metrics_v2.json are essentially identical for CED s43 except for the added note about the Ign-F1 fix, meaning the correction was already incorporated into the original evaluation rather than applied afterward. The worker name's reference to "fix" likely points to ensuring all evaluations follow the unified D076 protocol and that metrics_v2.json gets generated properly.

Looking at the overall work, the main contributions were creating structured comparison files and identifying which seeds needed reevaluation under the D076 protocol. However, there are some lingering issues: the summary file contains outdated notes about missing metrics files that actually exist, and the paper table still mixes batch sizes from different protocol versions until the reeval completes.

On the GPU front, I'm reconsidering whether the concurrent eval processes on cuda:0 are actually problematic. The constraint about keeping memory under 2GB was written after previous OOM failures, but with 32GB available on the RTX 5090, two processes using ~11GB each should fit. That said, the 84% utilization does create compute contention that slows both down, though this situation predates the worker's involvement since those reevals were already queued.

Now I'm noticing something more immediate: the worker submitted `reeval_sft_baseline_d076` at 05:26 UTC on cuda:1, but that GPU already shows ~10GB in use. That's potentially another C001 violation. Though I need to be careful here — the nvidia-smi snapshot I'm looking at is current state, not what the GPU memory looked like at submission time. The memory could have been freed or allocated differently when the job actually started.

Let me stick to what I can actually verify rather than guessing about past GPU states. The worker did solid work on the comparison files and caught some protocol inconsistencies, but there are some concerns with stale notes in the d076_seed_summary and the mixed-protocol data in paper_table_data. I should write up the review now.

oracle ceiling 的 37% (0.0807/0.218)。CED-F1 Spearman r=0.3235 (p≈0)，相关性显著但中等——说明 CED 不是完美的 proxy 但有信号。Random baseline rel_f1=0.4538 说明 sampling diversity 本身就有 +4.8pp 收益，CED 在此基础上再加 +3.3pp。EDCR 下降 (0.6156 vs SFT 0.7118)，N=8 sampling 引入更多 distractor citations。推理耗时 5.8h (N=8 生成主导)。结论：CED 作为 inference-time reranker 有明确论文价值——零训练成本超越 RSFT，且 oracle ceiling 表明更好的 reward model 还有大幅空间。\"}, Looking at the timeline, the first worker started at 05:25:36 and generated the seed summary around 05:22 UTC, while the second worker started at 05:25:47 and likely created or updated the metrics_v2.json files. But the timestamps seem off if the first worker generated the summary before the second worker finished. So the seed_comparison files generated at 05:25 UTC came from Worker 1, which started at 05:25:36. The real question is whether Worker 2 actually modified the metrics_v2.json files, or if they were already present from the initial eval run. Looking at the file structure, metrics_v2.json is nearly identical to metrics.json except for a note field, suggesting Worker 2 may have just recomputed the Ign-F1 fix on existing predictions rather than creating new files.

What's interesting is that when I compared the metrics for CED s43, the ign_f1 values were already identical between the two versions, meaning the recompute either verified already-correct files or regenerated ones that matched perfectly.

Worker 3 was doing preparation work for the seed comparison — the registry shows a `seed_comparison_prep` entry that was created much earlier at 02:35:52 UTC. The notes contain a pre-analysis of available results across different seeds, with flat-NLI RSFT results documented.

Compiling the performance metrics across seeds...

Looking at the pre-comparison results with just three completed seeds, CED RSFT is slightly outperforming flat-NLI by 0.0055 in relative F1, but the paired t-test shows this difference isn't statistically significant. I'm noticing the results are from older evaluations that predate the unified D076 protocol — the CED s42 checkpoint used different batch size and token settings, and the flat-NLI results are similarly outdated. The registry shows this was marked as preparing at 02:35 UTC, but the worker that started at 05:26 may have already refreshed these numbers.

Let me verify whether the seed comparison prep entry was actually updated since then or if I'm looking at stale data. the worker started. So the worker may not have updated the registry entry, or may have done its work differently (perhaps generating local files or analysis).

Given that:

3. Registry hasn't been updated since

I think these two workers did supporting work that was already partially reflected in the state I reviewed. The key findings from my first review still stand:
- Metrics are consistent
- Protocol mismatch in paper_table_data.json (SFT baseline at bs=16)
- d076_seed_summary notes are stale (now possibly even more stale if Worker 2 regenerated files)

attn_implementation=\\\"sdpa\\\",\\n        device_map=\\\"auto\\\",\\n    )\\n    if quantize:\\n        model_kwargs[\\\"quantization_config\\\"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(\\n \\\"user\\\", \\\"content\\\": f\\\"{instruction}\\\\n\\\\n{input_text}\\\"},\\n    ]\\n    return tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\\n        messages,\\n        tokenize=False,\\n        add_generation_prompt=True,\\n        enable_thinking=False,\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef prepare_eval_data(data_dir, split, tokenizer, include_evidence=True, system_prompt=SYSTEM_PROMPT):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"加载
